Al-Wartajbī writes that God reproached people whose knowledge remains merely outward, who chose worldly leadership, rejected the people of sanctity (ahl al-wilāya), preferred the fellowship of their opponents, and followed the impulses of their lower souls—their jibt—and the tracks of their ṭāghūt, Satan. Here ends his statement.

Ibn ʿAjība adds that the reproach extends to those who prefer the outward people (ahl al-ẓāhir) to the inward people (ahl al-bāṭin) and rank scholars above the friends of God (awliyāʾ), saying “These are better guided.” Far from it, he exclaims: the distance between them is like that between earth and heaven. He carefully specifies that the comparison is between knowers of God (ʿārifūn bi-llāh) who unite effacement (fanāʾ) and subsistence (baqāʾ), on the one side, and scholars and the God-fearing on the other. As for ordinary worshippers, ascetics and righteous people, he has no doubt that God-fearing scholars are superior to them.

He cites the prophetic saying: “The superiority of the scholar over the worshipper is as my superiority over the least of you,” together with other reports favouring the learned. Those who know God, however, are the greatest scholars, because their knowledge concerns God's very Essence through unveiling (kashf) and tasting (dhawq), whereas the scholars of the outward disciplines know God's rulings. Their attention is dispersed away from God (mufarraqūn ʿan Allāh); indeed, he says, they are more veiled from God than others. A saintly person reportedly said: “The people most veiled from God are first the scholars, then the worshippers, then the ascetics,” because delight in their present achievements prevents them from moving beyond them. Ibn ʿAjība refers readers to his earlier discussion of “You were the best community brought forth for humankind” [Q 3:110]. God knows best.

Transition ·

The next verse replies to the claim that political rule will pass to these opponents.

أَلَمْ تَرَ إِلَى الَّذِينَ أُوتُوا نَصِيباً مِنَ الْكِتابِ يُؤْمِنُونَ بِالْجِبْتِ وَالطَّاغُوتِ وَيَقُولُونَ لِلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا هؤُلاءِ أَهْدى مِنَ الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا سَبِيلاً (51) أُولئِكَ الَّذِينَ لَعَنَهُمُ اللَّهُ وَمَنْ يَلْعَنِ اللَّهُ فَلَنْ تَجِدَ لَهُ نَصِيراً (52) قلت: الجبت في الأصل: اسم صنم، فاستعمل في كل ما عُبد من دون الله، والطاغوت: كل باطل من معبود أو غيره، أو الجبت: السحر، والطاغوت: الساحر، وبالجملة: هو كل ما عُبد أو أطيع من دون الله، وقال الجوهري: الجبت: اسم لكل صنم ولكل عاصٍ ولكل ساحر وكل مُضِلّ، والطاغوت: الشيطان، وأصله: طغيوت، فعلوت، من الطغيان، ثم قلب فصار طيَغوت، ثم قلبت الياء ألفًا. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: أَلَمْ تَرَ إِلَى الَّذِينَ أُوتُوا نَصِيباً مِنَ علم الْكِتابِ، وهم أحبار اليهود يُؤْمِنُونَ بِالْجِبْتِ وَالطَّاغُوتِ يقرون بصحة عبادتهما، وَيَقُولُونَ لِلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا هؤُلاءِ الكفرة أَهْدى مِنَ الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا طريقًا، نزلت في اليهود- لعنهم الله-: كانوا يقولون: إن عبادة الأصنام أرضى عند الله مما يدعو إليه محمد صلّى الله عليه وسلم، وقيل: في حُيي بن أخطب وكعب بن الأشرف، خرجا في سبعين راكبًا إلى مكة يُحالفون قريشًا على محاربة رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم بعد وقعة أُحد، وينقضون العهد الذي كان بينهم وبين رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم، فنزل كَعبُ على أبي سفيان، فأحسن مثواه، ونزلت اليهود في دور قريش. فقال أهل مكة: أنتم أهل كتاب، ومحمدٌ صاحب كتاب، ولا نأمن أن يكون هذا مكيدة منكم، فإن أردتم أن نخرج معكم فاسجدوا لهذين الصنمين، وآمنوا بهما، ففعلوا، فذلك قوله تعالى: يُؤْمِنُونَ بِالْجِبْتِ وَالطَّاغُوتِ. ثم قال أبو سفيان لكعب: إنك امرؤ تقرأ الكتاب وتعلم، ونحن أميون لا نعلم، فأيُّنا أهدى سبيلاً وأقرب إلى الحق، نحن أو محمد؟ قال كعب: اعرضوا عليَّ دينَكم، فقال أبو سفيان: نحن ننحر للحجيج الكومَاءَ- أي: العظيمة- من النوق- ونسقي الماء، ونَقري الضيف، ونفك العاني، ونصل الرحم، ونعمر بيت ربنا، ونطوف به، ونحن أهل الحرم، ومحمدٌ فارقَ دينَ آبائه، وقطع الرحم وفارق الحرم، فقال كعب: أنتم والله أهدى سبيلاً. هـ. أُولئِكَ الَّذِينَ لَعَنَهُمُ اللَّهُ وأبعدهم وأسحقهم، وَمَنْ يَلْعَنِ اللَّهُ فَلَنْ تَجِدَ لَهُ نَصِيراً ينصره من عذاب الله. فقد قُتل هؤلاء كلهم شر قتلة، وذهبوا إلى الهاوية. عائذاً بالله. الإشارة: قال الورتجبي: وبَّخ الله تعالى أهل ظاهر العلم الذين اختاروا الرياسة، وأنكروا على أهل الولاية، وآثروا صحبة المخالفين، يقبلون هواجس نفَوسهم التي هي الجبت، ويخطون على آثار الطاغوت، التي هي إبليس. هـ. قلت: وينسحب التوبيخ على من فضّل أهل الظاهر على أهل الباطن، وفضَّل العلماء على الأولياء، ويقولون: هم أهدى منهم سبيلاً. هيهات! بينهم من البَون ما بين السماء والأرض. والكلام إنما هو في التفضيل بين العارفين بالله، الذين جمعوا بين الفناء والبقاء، وبين العلماء والأتقياء. وأما العُبَّاد والزهاد والصالحون فلا شك أن العلماء الأتقياء أفضل منهم، وإليهم أشار صلّى الله عليه وسلم بقوله: «فَضلُ العَالِمِ على العابِد كَفَضِلِي عَلَى أدناكُم» . وكذلك الأحاديث التي وردت في تفضيل العلماء. وأما العارفون بالله فهم أعظم العلماء، لأن علمهم متعلق بذات الله كشفًا وذوقًا، وعلماء الظاهر علمهم متعلق بأحكام الله. مفرقُون عن الله، بل هم أشد حجابًا من غيرهم عن الله. قال بعض الأولياء: أشد الناس حجابًا عن الله: العلماء ثم العباد ثم الزهاد. هـ. لأن حلاوة ما هم فيه تمنعهم عن الانتقال عنه، وقد تقدم الكلام عند قوله: كُنْتُمْ خَيْرَ أُمَّةٍ أُخْرِجَتْ لِلنَّاسِ (١) بأبلغ من هذا. والله تعالى أعلم. ثم ردّ الحق تعالى على اليهود، حيث ادعوا أن الملك سيصير إليهم، فقال:

4:53 · 4:53 · Sovereignty, stinginess, and the gifts of the self

Cairo I:514–515

“Or do they possess a share in the dominion? If so, they would not give people even a speck.”

Language ·

Am is disconnected (munqaṭiʿa), equivalent to “rather,” with the interrogative hamza expressing rejection. It denies the claim—attributed here to Jews—that sovereignty will become theirs. When idhan is separated from a present-tense verb by lā, both operation and non-operation of the particle are allowed; the reading wa-idhan lā yalbathū is cited by way of comparison. A naqīr is the little pit on the back of a date-stone; it serves here as an image of the smallest possible gift, exposing extreme miserliness.

Exposition ·

Ibn ʿAjība paraphrases the divine reproach: Could they obtain any part of dominion or public authority? Far from it! Such a thing will never happen, he writes in this polemical context. How, he asks, can power be theirs when they are so miserly? Even given a share of sovereignty, they would not bestow a naqīr upon other people, let alone something greater. In Ibn ʿAjība's moral argument, political authority and victory depend on generosity, open-handedness, courage, sound judgment and good government: he portrays the people criticized in the verse as lacking these virtues.

Spiritual ·

God grants strength, victory and the power to act (taṣarruf), outwardly or inwardly, only to the generous. One who gives freely of his wealth without being concerned about how much he gives or to whom is enabled to enjoy outward honour and effective action (taṣarruf ḥissī). One who gives away his own self and standing (nafs and jāh) and expends both in pursuit of his Lord's good pleasure is granted spiritual dignity, victory and inward effective action (taṣarruf maʿnawī): his spiritual aspiration (himma) acts throughout existence, from the Throne to the earth beneath it. His honour and victory endure perpetually. God knows best.

Transition ·

As envy and miserliness are two especially blameworthy vices, the exposition proceeds to reproach envy as well.

أَمْ لَهُمْ نَصِيبٌ مِنَ الْمُلْكِ فَإِذاً لا يُؤْتُونَ النَّاسَ نَقِيراً (53) قلت: «أم» : منقطعة، بمعنى بل، والهمزة للإنكار، وهو إنكار وجحدٌ لما زعمت اليهود من أنَّ المُلكَ سيصيرُ لهم، و (إذًا) إن فُصل بينها وبين المضارع ب- «لا» ففيها الإهمال والإعمال، وقد قرئ: (وإذًا لا يلبثوا) ، والنقير: النقرة التي في ظهر النواة، وهو هنا كناية عن نهاية بخلهم. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله منكرا على اليهود: أيحصل لهم نَصِيبٌ مِنَ الْمُلْكِ والرياسة؟ هيهات، لا يكون هذا أبدًا، فكيف يكون لهم الملك وهم أبخل الناس؟. فإذا أُوتوا شيئًا من الملك لا يُعطون الناس نقيرًا، فما بالك بأكثر، والملك والنصر لا يكونان إلا لأجل الكرم والجود والشجاعة، وإصابة الرأي وحسن التدبير، وهم بعداء من هذه المكارم. الإشارة: لا يُمكن اللهُ من العز والنصر والتصرف الظاهر أو الباطن إلا أهل السخاء والجود، فمن جاد بمالِهِ حتى لا يبالي كم أعطى ولا لمن أعطى، مكّنه الله من العز والتصرف الحسي، ومن جاد بنفسه وجاهه، وبذلهما في مرضاة ربه، مكّنه الله من العز والنصر والتصرف المعنوي يتصرف بهمته في الوجود بأسره، من عرشه إلى فرشه، ويدوم عزه ونصره إلى أبد الأبد. والله تعالى أعلم. ولمّا كان الحسد والبخل رذيلتين متناهيتين فى الذم وصفهم الحق- تعالى- أيضا به (١) ، فقال:

4:54–57 · 4:54–57 · Envy, divine favour, fire, and the shelter of contentment

Cairo I:515–517

“Or do they envy people for what God has bestowed upon them of His bounty? We gave the House of Abraham the Book and wisdom, and We bestowed upon them a mighty dominion. Among them are those who believe in him and those who turn away from him; Hell is sufficient as a blazing fire. Those who disbelieve in Our signs—We shall make them enter a fire; whenever their skins are consumed, We shall replace them with other skins so that they may taste the punishment. God is Mighty, Wise. But those who believe and do righteous deeds—We shall admit them to gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding therein for ever. There they shall have purified spouses, and We shall bring them into abundant, sheltering shade.”

Language ·

The particle am here has the force of “rather,” and saʿīran (“blazing fire”) is a specification (tamyīz).

Exposition ·

In rebuking envy, God says: Do they envy “people”—that is, the Arabs, to whom prophethood has now come after being among the ancestors of those addressed—for the bounty He has given them? The bounty is the manifestation of prophethood among them. Or “people” can mean the Messenger himself, peace be upon him, in whom have been gathered qualities that are separately distributed among other people. According to the story Ibn ʿAjība transmits, his opponents envied him for the prophethood and other favours God had bestowed and said, disparagingly: “He has no concern but women; if he were a prophet, the labour of prophethood would occupy him rather than women.”

God contradicts them: “We gave the House of Abraham”—Ibn ʿAjība names Joseph, David and Solomon—“the Book and wisdom,” which he here explains as prophethood, “and bestowed upon them a mighty dominion.” He then relates numerical claims that David had one hundred women, and Solomon one thousand: three hundred wives married with a dower (mahīra) and seven hundred concubines. He reports the Prophet as answering those critics, when the verse descended, by comparing these numbers with his nine wives, after which they fell silent. [Translator’s note: The numerical claims belong to reports cited in the commentator's argument; their historical accuracy is unestablished.]

“Among them”—meaning the Jews—“are those who believe in him,” namely Muḥammad, such as ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salām and his companions, and others who turn away from him. Alternatively, the pronouns concern the House of Abraham: some believed in Abraham, others rejected him; just as this did not diminish Abraham's standing, their rejection does not diminish your mission. A third interpretation contrasts earlier people who accepted the Book, wisdom and kingship given to Abraham's family with others who rejected them, as they had rejected Solomon and others. “Hell is sufficient as a blazing fire” for those who reject what any messenger brings. If their punishment is not hastened in the present world, the fire reserved for them is sufficient.

God then describes the end of those who reject His revealed signs or the signs of His unity. “We shall make them enter fire”: that is, burn and roast them. “Whenever their skins are consumed,” softened and burned, “We replace them with other skins.” Ibn ʿAjība quotes a saying attributed to the Prophet that they are changed a hundred times in an hour. Al-Ḥasan is cited as saying that the fire consumes them seventy thousand times a day; every time it consumes and cooks them, they are told to return and return as they were. Mujāhid is cited as describing worms between skin and flesh, in commotion and growling like wild asses. From Abū Hurayra Ibn ʿAjība transmits the prophetic report: “The thickness of an unbeliever's skin will be forty-two cubits, and his molar tooth like Uḥud.”

The replacement of skins makes the pain endure, by creating other skin in the place of what was consumed. The actual subject of punishment, he observes, is the disobedient soul and not the instrument through which it perceives; therefore he sees no difficulty in this replacement. God is “Mighty”: none can prevent Him from acting as He wills; and “Wise”: He punishes in accordance with His wisdom.

God then contrasts the destiny of the believers. Those who believe and act rightly enter gardens with flowing rivers and abide there for ever; their spouses are purified of everything repellent; and they enjoy a shade that is perpetual, never removed by sunshine and never accompanied by cold. The warning to unbelievers comes first because they are the immediate subject of the discourse, while the believers are mentioned by contrast. God knows best.

Spiritual ·

Envy (ḥasad) is a blameworthy disposition from which only the most truthful (ṣiddīqūn) become completely purified. Anyone in whom a trace of envy remains cannot smell the fragrance of true knowing (maʿrifa). Had the person known God, there would be no object of envy left to him. The saying goes: “The envious person never rises to leadership” (al-ḥasūd lā yasūd). Ibn ʿAjība quotes as a prophetic saying: “Envy consumes good deeds as fire consumes firewood.” Sufyān also reports that it reached him that God says: “The envious person is an enemy of My blessing, dissatisfied with the portion I have divided among My servants.”

Poets have said:

Say to one who envies me: Do you know against whom you have offended in courtesy?
You have offended against God in His act, since you disapprove of what He has given me.
Your recompense from Him is an increase for me, and that you will not obtain what you seek.

And another:

If you envy me, I do not blame you: before my time, those of excellence were envied.
May what is in me and in them remain with each of us; and may the greater number of us die from the anger they feel.

[Translator’s note: The poet wishes that his merit and their envy should persist, as al-Fārisī explains in his commentary on the Ḥamāsa, p. 224. Al-Marzūqī, Sharḥ Dīwān al-Ḥamāsa, I:294, takes “the greater number of us” as the enviers, who are many against one. Another grammatical construal of aktharunā ghayẓan is “the one of us most enraged.” The wish and the parties’ different conditions are clear; the latter construction is not treated here as uniquely determined.]

Enmity born from envy cannot be removed and is not benefited by treatment: the envious person oppresses another yet complains as though oppressed. A poet rightly said:

Every hostility may admit the hope of removal,
except the hostility of one who opposes you out of envy.

And a master of poets said:

The most unjust of God's creatures is one who lies awake in envy
of a person who lies surrounded by His blessings.

And another:

I pity my enviers for all the rancour their breasts contain.
They saw God's handiwork in me: their eyes were in a garden,
while their hearts were in a fire.

A wise person said that the envious inflicts three kinds of injury on himself. First, he accumulates sins, for envy is forbidden. Second, he violates adab toward God, since envy is essentially dislike of God's giving blessings to someone else and an objection to the divine act. Third, he suffers pain of heart, recurring distress and sorrow. May God protect us from all this. The envious person remains in the fire of veiling (nār al-ḥijāb) and the anguish of accounting (ghamm al-ḥisāb). The one purified of envy enters the garden of contentment (riḍā) and surrender (taslīm) in the neighbourhood of the Beloved: the place of rest and security in both abodes, the “abundant shade” of the verse. God knows best.

Transition ·

Since the order of religion cannot be maintained without struggle (jihād), and such struggle cannot be organized without appointing an imam, God turns first to what concerns rulers, and then later speaks of struggle.

أَمْ يَحْسُدُونَ النَّاسَ عَلى ما آتاهُمُ اللَّهُ مِنْ فَضْلِهِ فَقَدْ آتَيْنا آلَ إِبْراهِيمَ الْكِتابَ وَالْحِكْمَةَ وَآتَيْناهُمْ مُلْكاً عَظِيماً (54) فَمِنْهُمْ مَنْ آمَنَ بِهِ وَمِنْهُمْ مَنْ صَدَّ عَنْهُ وَكَفى بِجَهَنَّمَ سَعِيراً (55) إِنَّ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا بِآياتِنا سَوْفَ نُصْلِيهِمْ ناراً كُلَّما نَضِجَتْ جُلُودُهُمْ بَدَّلْناهُمْ جُلُوداً غَيْرَها لِيَذُوقُوا الْعَذابَ إِنَّ اللَّهَ كانَ عَزِيزاً حَكِيماً (56) وَالَّذِينَ آمَنُوا وَعَمِلُوا الصَّالِحاتِ سَنُدْخِلُهُمْ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهارُ خالِدِينَ فِيها أَبَداً لَهُمْ فِيها أَزْواجٌ مُطَهَّرَةٌ وَنُدْخِلُهُمْ ظِلاًّ ظَلِيلاً (57) قلت: (أم) بمعنى بل، و (سعيرًا) تمييز. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله توبيخًا لليهود على الحسد: أَمْ يَحْسُدُونَ النَّاسَ، أي: العرب حيث انتقلت النبوة إليهم، وقد كانت في أسلافهم، عَلى ما آتاهُمُ اللَّهُ مِنْ فَضْلِهِ، وهو ظهور النبوة فيهم، أو رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم لأنه اجتمع فيه ما افترق في سائر الناس، حسدوه على ما آتاه الله من فضله، من النبوة وغيرها، وقالوا- لعنهم الله-: ماله همٌّ إلا النساء، ولو كان نبيًا لشغله أمر النبوة عن النساء. فكَّذبهم الله- تعالى- وردَّ عليهم بقوله: فَقَدْ آتَيْنا آلَ إِبْراهِيمَ وهم: يوسف وداود وسليمان، الْكِتابَ وَالْحِكْمَةَ أي: النبوّة، وَآتَيْناهُمْ مُلْكاً عَظِيماً، فقد اجتمع لداود عليه السلام مائة امرأة. ولسليمان- عليه السلام- ألف امرأة: ثلاثمائة مهيرة، - أي بالمهر- وسبعمائة سرية، فقال لهم- عليه الصلاة والسلام- حين نزلت الآية: ألف امرأة عند رجل، ومائة امرأة عند آخر، أكثر من تسع نسوة، فسكتوا (٢) . فَمِنْهُمْ أي: اليهود، مَنْ آمَنَ بِهِ أي: بمحمد- عليه الصلاة والسلام- كعبد الله بن سلام وأصحابه، وَمِنْهُمْ مَنْ صَدَّ عَنْهُ أي: أعرض عنه، أو: من آل إبراهيم من آمن بإبراهيم، ومنهم من صدّ عنه، ولم يكن في ذلك توهين لقدر إبراهيم، فكذلك لا يُوهن كفرُ هؤلاء أمرَك، أو: من أسلافهم من آمن بما أُوتي آل إبراهيم من الكتاب والحكمة والمُلك، ومنهم مَن صدّ عنه، كما فعلوا مع سليمان وغيره. وَكَفى بِجَهَنَّمَ سَعِيراً لمن كفر بما جاء به أحد من الرسل، أي: فإن لم يُعاجَلوا بالعقوبة فقد كفاهم ما أعد لهم من سعير جهنم. ثم بيَّن مآل مَن كفر، فقال: إِنَّ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا بِآياتِنا المنزلة على رسلنا، أو الدالة على وحدانيتنا، سَوْفَ نُصْلِيهِمْ نارًا أي: نحرقهم بها ونشويهم، كُلَّما نَضِجَتْ جُلُودُهُمْ أي: لانت واحترقت بَدَّلْناهُمْ جُلُوداً غَيْرَها، قال صلّى الله عليه وسلم: «تُبدَّلُ في ساعةٍ مائةَ مَرَّة» . وقال الحسن: (تأكلهم النارُ في كل يوم سبعين ألف مرة، كلما أكلتهم وأنضجتهم قيل لهم: عودوا فيعودون كما كانوا) . وقال مجاهد: (ما بين جلده ولحمه دود، لها جلبة- أي حركة- وهرير كجلبة حمر الوحش) . رَوى أبو هريرة رضي الله عنه عن النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم قال: «غلظٌ جِلدِ الكَافِرِ اثنَانٍ وأربعَونَ ذرَاعًا، وضِرسه مِثلُ أحُد» . وإنما بدلت جلودهم لِيَذُوقُوا ألم الْعَذابَ، أي: يدوم لهم ذلك بخلق جلد آخر مكانه، والعذاب في الحقيقة للنفس العاصية لا لآلة إدراكها، فلا محذور، إِنَّ اللَّهَ كانَ عَزِيزاً لا يمتنع عليه ما يريد، حَكِيماً يعاقب على قدر حكمته. ثم ذكر مقابل هؤلاء فقال: وَالَّذِينَ آمَنُوا وَعَمِلُوا الصَّالِحاتِ سَنُدْخِلُهُمْ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهارُ خالِدِينَ فِيها أَبَداً لَهُمْ فِيها أَزْواجٌ مُطَهَّرَةٌ مما يستقذر وَنُدْخِلُهُمْ ظِلًّا ظَلِيلًا أي: دائمًا لا تنسخه شمس، ولا يصحبه برد. قدَّم وعيد الكفار على وعد المؤمنين، لأن الكلام فيهم، وذكر المؤمنين بالعرض. والله تعالى أعلم. الإشارة: الحسد خلق مذموم، لا يتطهر منه إلا الصديقون، وكل من بقي فيه بقية من الحسد لا يشم رائحة المعرفة، إذ لو عرف الله لم يجد من يحسد، وقد قيل: الحسود لا يسود. وفي الحديث عنه صلّى الله عليه وسلم أنه قال: «الحَسدُ يأكُلُ الحسنَاتِ كما تأكلُ النَّارُ الحَطَبَ» . وقال سفيان: (بلغني إنَّ الله تبارك وتعالى يقول: الحاسدُ عدو نعمتي، غيرُ راضٍ بقسمتي التي قسمت بين عبادي) . وأنشدوا: ألاَ قُل لمَن كانَ لي حَاسِدًا ... أتدري على مَن أسَأتَ الأدَب أسَأتَ على اللهِ في فِعلِه ... إذا أنتَ لم تَرضَ لي مَا وَهَب جَزَاؤُكَ منه الزيادةُ لي ... وألاَّ تَنَالَ الذِي تَطَّلِب وقال آخر: إن تحسُدُوني فإني غيرُ لائمكم ... قَبَلى من الناسِ أهل الفضلِ قّد حُسِدُوا فَدَامَ لي وَلَهُم ما كَانَ بي وبهم ... وماتَ أكثَرُنا غيظًا بما يَجِدُ ثم إن الحسود لا تزول عداواته، ولا تنفع مداواته، وهو ظالم يشتكي كأنه مظلوم. ولقد صدق القائل: كلُ العَدَاوةِ قد تُرجىَ إزالتُها ... إلا عداوة مَن عَادَاكَ مِن حَسَدٍ وقال حكيم الشعراء: وأظلمُ خَلقِ اللهِ مَن بَاتَ حَاسِدًا ... لِمَن بَاتَ في نَعمَائِه يَتَقَلَّبُ وقال آخر: إني لأرحمُ حاسِدِيَّ لفرطِ ما ... ضَمَّت صُدُوُرُهُم مِن الأوغَارِ نَظَرُوا صنيعَ اللهِ فيّ فَعُيُونهُم ... في جَنَّةٍ وقَلوبُهُم في نارِ قال بعض الحكماء: (الحاسدُ يضرُّ نفسه ثلاث مضرات: إحداها: اكتساب الذنوب لأن الحسد حرام. الثانية: سوء الأدب مع الله- تعالى- فإنَّ حقيقة الحسد: كراهية إنعام الله على غيره، واعتراض على الله في فعله. الثالثة: تألم قلبه وكثرة همه وغمه) . عافانا الله من ذلك كله، فالحاسد لا ينفك عن نار الحجاب وغم الحساب، والمتطهر منه يدخل جنة الرضى والتسليم في جوار الحبيب، وهو محل الراحة والأمن في الدارين، وهو الظل الظليل. والله تعالى أعلم. ولما كان حفظ نظام الدين لا يقوم إلا بالجهاد، ولا ينتظم الجهاد إلا بنصب الإمام، تكلم الحق- جل جلاله- على ما يتعلق بالأمراء، ثم بعد ذلك يتكلم على الجهاد، فقال:

4:58 · 4:58 · The trust of government and the trust of spiritual knowledge

Cairo I:517–518

“God commands you to deliver trusts to those entitled to them and, when you judge between people, to judge justly. Excellent indeed is what God admonishes you with. God has always been All-Hearing, All-Seeing.”

Language ·

In the expression niʿimmā (“excellent indeed is what ...”), mā may be a specifying accusative (tamyīz) or the grammatical subject, while what is specially commended is unexpressed. Thus it is either “excellent is the thing with which He admonishes you” or “excellent is that with which He admonishes you: namely, this command.” The commanded good is the restoration of trusts and equity in adjudication.

Exposition · Occasion and scope

Zayd ibn Aslam and Shahr ibn Ḥawshab are cited as saying that the verse concerns rulers. Ibn ʿAjība adds that even if its occasion was ʿUthmān ibn Ṭalḥa, custodian (sādin) of the Kaʿba, it is general in scope. The one first addressed is the Messenger, peace be upon him, the foremost of rulers. God commanded him to return the keys to ʿUthmān. Ibn ʿAjība relates that at the conquest of Mecca ʿUthmān had locked the Kaʿba's door and refused to hand over the keys so the Messenger might enter, saying, “Had I known he was God's Messenger, I would not have prevented him.” ʿAlī then twisted his arm and took the keys. The Messenger entered and prayed two cycles. When he emerged, al-ʿAbbās requested the keys, so that custody of the Kaʿba (sidāna) and the provision of water to pilgrims (siqāya) might be combined in him. God commanded that the keys be returned to their holder; the Prophet ordered ʿAlī to return them and apologise. The incident became the occasion of ʿUthmān's embracing Islam, and the guardianship, according to this account, was assigned to his descendants perpetually.

Exposition ·

God commands rulers to restore what has been entrusted to its rightful owners, whether among themselves or among their subjects. They must give redress to the oppressed against the oppressor, until the rights of people are discharged: a loan or debt, a deposit, goods seized by force or theft, or other claims among human beings. They must give zakāt to its lawful recipients and distribute the public treasury (bayt al-māl) to those entitled to it, neither wronging its beneficiaries nor diverting any of it to an undeserving purpose. They must judge with equity those who are subject to their jurisdiction. “God admonishes you most excellently”: His command is indeed excellent advice. God is All-Hearing and All-Seeing: none of their judgments nor any trusts they have concealed can escape Him.

Spiritual ·

God likewise commands the teachers of spiritual training (shuyūkh al-tarbiya) to hand over the secret (sirr) to those fuqarāʾ [disciples of the path] entitled to it, when the teachers have become certain of their readiness (ahliyya). This occurs when the aspirants have emptied themselves of vices such as envy and pride and have adorned themselves with the virtues: purity of heart, generous souls, good character, and other marks of completeness. Once takhliya and taḥliya have been realized in them, they become qualified to receive the secrets of lordship (asrār al-rubūbiyya), held in trust by the people of special spiritual election. Teachers must also judge justly between the fuqarāʾ, extending to each the measure of help appropriate to his truthfulness (ṣidq) and service (khidma). God knows best.

Transition ·

After commanding rulers to be just and to return trusts, God commands obedience to those rulers.

إِنَّ اللَّهَ يَأْمُرُكُمْ أَنْ تُؤَدُّوا الْأَماناتِ إِلى أَهْلِها وَإِذا حَكَمْتُمْ بَيْنَ النَّاسِ أَنْ تَحْكُمُوا بِالْعَدْلِ إِنَّ اللَّهَ نِعِمَّا يَعِظُكُمْ بِهِ إِنَّ اللَّهَ كانَ سَمِيعاً بَصِيراً (58) قلت: «ما» في (نِعِمَّا) تمييز أو فاعل، والمخصوص محذوف، أي نعم شيئًا شيء يعظكم به، أو نعم الذي يعظكم به ذلك الأمر، وهو رد الأمانات والعدل في الحكومات. قال زيد بن أسلم وشهر بن حوشب: نزلت الآية في شأن الأمراء. هـ قلت: وإن نزلت في شأن عثمان بن طلحة- سَادِن الكَعبَةِ فهي عامة. والمخاطب بذلك أولاً الرسول صلّى الله عليه وسلم وهو سيد الأمراء، أمره الحق- تعالى- أن يرد المفاتح إلى عثمان، وذلك أن عثمان أغلق باب الكعبة يوم فتح مكة، وأبى أن يدفعها إلى رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم ليدخل الكعبة، وقال: لو عَلمِتُ أنه رَسُول الله (١) ما منعتُهُ، فَلَوى عَلِيٌّ يده، وأخذها مِنهُ، فدخل رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم وصَلّى رَكعَتينِ، فلما خَرَجَ سألُه العباس أن يُعطيه المفتَاح، وَيَجمَعَ له السِّدَانَةَ والسِّقَايةَ، فأمره اللهُ- تعالى- أن يرده إليه، فأمر عَلِيًّا بأن يَرُدَّه وَيَعتَذِر إليه، وكان ذلك سببًا لإسلام عثمان، ونزل الأمر بأن السِّدَانَة في أولاده أبدًا. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: إِنَّ اللَّهَ يَأْمُرُكُمْ، يا معشر الأمراء، أن تردوا الْأَماناتِ إِلى أَهْلِها من أنفسكم، أو من رعيتكم فتُنصفوا المظلوم من الظالم، حتى يؤدي ما ائتُمن عليه من دَينٍ، أو وديعة، أو غصب، أو سرقة، أو غير ذلك من حقوق العباد، بعضهم من بعض، وأن تؤدوا الزكاة إلى من يستحقها، وتصرفوا بيت المال فيمن يستحقه، لا تظلموا أهلها، ولا تضيعوا منها شيئاً في غير مستحقها. وَيأمركم إِذا حَكَمْتُمْ بَيْنَ النَّاسِ أَنْ تَحْكُمُوا بِالْعَدْلِ في من يَنفُذُ عليه حكمُكُم، إِنَّ اللَّهَ نِعِمَّا يَعِظُكُمْ بِهِ أي: إن الله يعظكم بأمر نعم ما هو، إِنَّ اللَّهَ كانَ سَمِيعاً بَصِيراً لا يخفى عليه أحكامكم، ولا ما أخفيتم من أمانات غيركم. الإشارة: أمر الحق- جل جلاله- شيوخ التربية أن يؤدوا السر إلى من يستحقه من الفقراء، إذا تحققوا أهليتهم له، بحيث تخلوا عن الرذائل، كالحسد والكبر وغيرهما، وتحلوا بالفضائل، كسلامة الصدر وسخاوة النفوس وحسن الخلق، وغير ذلك من أوصاف الكمال، فإن تحققوا بالتخلية والتحلية، استحقوا الاطلاع على أسرار الربوبية، التي هي أمانات عند أهل الخصوصية، وأمرهم أن يحكموا بين الفقراء بالعدل، فيمدوا كلاً على قدر صِدقِه وخِدمَتِه، والله تعالى أعلم. ثم أمر الحق تعالى بطاعة الأمراء الذين أمرهم بالعدل وأداء الأمانة، فقال:

4:59 · 4:59 · Obedience, juristic reasoning, and the authority of the shaykh

Cairo I:518–520

“O believers, obey God, and obey the Messenger, and those entrusted with authority among you. If you dispute about anything, refer it back to God and the Messenger, if you believe in God and the Last Day. That is better and more excellent in its outcome.”

Language · Grammar and principle of interpretation

The governing verb “obey” is repeated before “the Messenger,” marking his independent right to obedience. It is not repeated before “those entrusted with authority,” indicating that there may be among them persons whose commands are not binding. This is then clarified by “If you dispute about anything,” as though the verse said: if they do not act in accordance with the truth, do not obey them in that matter; return the point of disagreement to the judgment of God and His Messenger. Ibn ʿAjība attributes this explanation to al-Ṭībī and promises to elaborate it.

Exposition ·

O believers, obey God in what He commands and forbids, and obey His Messenger likewise. “And those in authority among you” means those who administer your affairs, including just governors such as the caliphs and rulers who followed them. They must be obeyed in commands entailing obedience to God, not in sins, although Ibn ʿAjība makes an exception concerning the fear of civil disorder (harj). He adds that if a ruler does not act justly, obedience remains required for fear of sedition (fitna), and calls this the sounder position. The Prophet said: “Obedience is only in what is right” (innamā l-ṭāʿatu fī l-maʿrūf).

He also quotes a report attributed to Abū Hurayra: “Governors will rule you: the virtuous will govern through their virtue and the wicked through their wickedness. Listen to them and obey them in everything that accords with the truth; pray behind them. If they do well, the benefit is theirs; if they do wrong, it is for you [the merit of your proper conduct] and against them [the sin].”

Another ḥadīth says: “Unless you see manifest unbelief (kufr bawāḥ) for which you have a proof from God.” [Textual note: Cairo I:519 prints لا أن تروا, whereas the report in Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī, nos. 7055–7056, reads إلا أن تروا (“unless you see”). The English follows the latter reading here; the apparent omission in the Cairo print is expressly disclosed.] Ibn ʿAjība glosses this as requiring the removal of such rulers. He further quotes a question which the Cairo text attributes to Abū Wāʾil: “Messenger of God, what if we have rulers who withhold our rights but demand their own?” The Prophet replies: “Listen and obey; they bear the burden entrusted to them, and you bear the burden entrusted to you.” [Source note: Muslim 1846a names Salama b. Yazīd al-Juʿfī as the questioner, in a report transmitted through ʿAlqama b. Wāʾil from his father; 1846b explicitly assigns the reply to the Prophet. Al-Thaʿlabī on 4:59 likewise gives ʿAlqama’s report from his father, but leaves the questioner unnamed. This earlier tafsīr parallel separates narrator from questioner and supports Muslim’s report structure. Cairo’s Abū Wāʾil is retained as its own attribution; the exact origin of that different name is unestablished.]

Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh, al-Ḥasan, al-Ḍaḥḥāk and Mujāhid are also reported as holding that ulū l-amr signifies the jurists and scholars, people of religion and excellence, who teach the foundations of faith, command what is right and forbid what is wrong. The proof offered is “If they had referred it to the Messenger and to those in authority among them ...” [Q 4:83]. Abū l-Aswad remarked: “Nothing is dearer than knowledge. Kings judge their peoples, and scholars judge kings.”

“If you dispute”—whether between yourselves and rulers or amongst yourselves—over the ruling of a matter of religion, and you do not know what its judgment is, “refer it to God,” that is, to God's Book, and “the Messenger,” either to him during his lifetime or to his sunna after his death. If there is no explicit text, one returns it by analogy (qiyās). Rules therefore fall into three classes: established by the Book, established by the sunna, and established by being referred to both through a valid qiyās.

Ibn ʿAjība then cites from Ibrāhīm ibn Yasār a saying attributed to the Prophet: “Act according to the Qurʾān: deem lawful its lawful things, deem unlawful its forbidden things, believe in it, and reject none of it. Whatever is unclear to you, refer to God Most High and to those who possess knowledge after me, so that they may inform you of it.” The continuation reads: “Let the Qurʾān and the clarification within it suffice you, for it is an intercessor whose intercession is granted, an accuser whose testimony is accepted, and for every letter of it there is a light on the Day of Resurrection.” [Translator’s source note: An earlier close parallel in al-Thaʿlabī, al-Kashf wa-l-bayān, III:337, under Q4:59, introduces this report through “Abū al-Malīḥ al-Hudhalī, from Maʿqil ibn Yasār” (أبو المليح الهذلي عن معقل بن يسار). Cairo instead names Ibrāhīm ibn Yasār. The parallel locates the report but does not establish which attribution Ibn ʿAjība wrote or authenticate the transmitted saying.]

Refer the rulings, then, to God and the Messenger “if you believe in God and the Last Day,” for faith itself demands this. Such referral is better for you and “more excellent in taʾwīl”—better than applying your own interpretation without reference to revelation, and better in final consequence and issue. God knows best.

Spiritual ·

For the Sufis, those charged with authority (ulū l-amr) are the spiritual masters who know God (shuyūkh al-tarbiya al-ʿārifūn bi-llāh). Aspirants (murīdūn) are obliged, within the discipline of the path, to obey them in ease and hardship and in everything they instruct. Ibn ʿAjība says that one who objects or asks “Why?” does not prosper, and that for perceptive aspirants a gesture suffices without a fully explicit command.

If a command of rulers conflicts with a command of the shaykhs, the disciple, according to Ibn ʿAjība, gives precedence to the shaykh, except when doing so would occasion a grave public disturbance: the shaykh himself then commands obedience to rulers to avoid disorder affecting the fuqarāʾ. Should you, people of the path, dispute about a question of outward law (sharīʿa) or the inner way (ṭarīqa), refer it to the Book and the sunna. Al-Junayd said: “This path of ours is supported by the Book and the sunna. Whoever has not read the Qurʾān and learned the ḥadīth is not to be followed in this affair.” For each person, the essential portion is what his acts of worship require. God knows best.

Transition ·

God next speaks of those who turn away from His and His Messenger's judgment and consent to judgment by another.

يا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا أَطِيعُوا اللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُوا الرَّسُولَ وَأُولِي الْأَمْرِ مِنْكُمْ فَإِنْ تَنازَعْتُمْ فِي شَيْءٍ فَرُدُّوهُ إِلَى اللَّهِ وَالرَّسُولِ إِنْ كُنْتُمْ تُؤْمِنُونَ بِاللَّهِ وَالْيَوْمِ الْآخِرِ ذلِكَ خَيْرٌ وَأَحْسَنُ تَأْوِيلاً (59) أعاد العامل في قوله: (وأطيعوا الرسول) ، إشارة إلى استقلال الرسول بالطاعة، ولم يُعده في أُولِي الْأَمْرِ إشارة إلى أنه يوجد منهم من لا تجب طاعته، ثم بيّنه بقوله: فَإِنْ تَنازَعْتُمْ فِي شَيْءٍ كأنه قيل: فإن لم يعملوا بالحق فلا تطيعوهم، وردوا ما تخالفتم فيه إلى حكم الله ورسوله. قاله الطيبي، وسيأتي تحريرُ ذلك إن شاء الله تعالى. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: يا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا أَطِيعُوا اللَّهَ فيما أمركم به ونهاكم عنه، وَأَطِيعُوا الرَّسُولَ كذلك. وَأُولِي الْأَمْرِ مِنْكُمْ أي: مَن ولي أمرَكم. من وُلاَةِ العدل كالخلفاء والأمراء بعدهم، تجب طاعتهم فيما أمَروُا به من الطاعة دون المعصية إلا لخوف هرج. قال عليه الصلاة والسلام: «إنَّمَا الطَّاعَةُ فِي المَعرُوف» ، فإن لم يعدل: وجبت طاعته خوفًا من الفتنة. وهذا هو الأصح. لقوله- عليه الصلاة والسلام-: «سَيَليكم ولاة، فيليكم البَرُّ ببره، والفاجر بفجوره، فاستمعوا لهم، وأطيعوا في كل ما وافق الحق، فصلوا وراءهم، فإن أحسنوا فلهم، وإن أساءوا فلكم وعليهم» . رواه أبو هريرة. وفي حديث آخر: «لا أن تَروا كُفرًا بَوَاحًا، لكم عليه من اللهِ بُرهَانٌ» . أي: فيجب عزلهم. وقال أيضا صلّى الله عليه وسلم لما سأله أبو وائل فقال: يا رسول الله أرأيت إن كان علينا أمراء يمنعوننا حقنا ويسألون حقهم؟. فقال صلّى الله عليه وسلم: «اسمَعُوا وأطِيعُوا، فَإنَّ عليهم ما حُمِّلُوا وَعَليكُم ما حُملتم» . وقال جابر بن عبد الله والحسن والضحاك ومجاهد: أولو الأمر هم الفقهاء والعلماء، أهل الدين والفضل، يُعلَّمون الناس معالم دينهم، ويأمرون بالمعروف وينْهون عن المنكر، دليله. قوله تعالى: وَلَوْ رَدُّوهُ إِلَى الرَّسُولِ وَإِلى أُولِي الْأَمْرِ مِنْهُمْ ... الآية. قال أبو الأسود: ليس شيء أعز من العلم، الملوك حُكَّام على الناس، والعلماء حُكَّام على الملوك. هـ. فَإِنْ تَنازَعْتُمْ أنتم وأولو الأمر، أو بعضكم مع بعض- أي: اختلفتم في حكم شيء من أمر الدين فلم تعلموا حكمه، فَرُدُّوهُ إِلَى اللَّهِ أي: إلى كتاب الله، وَإلى الرَّسُولَ في زمانه، أو سنته بعد موته، فإن لم يوجد بالنص فبالقياس. فالأحكام ثلاثة: مثبت بالكتاب، ومثبت بالسنة، ومثبت بالرد إليهما على وجه القياس. وعن إبراهيم بن يسار قال: قال النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: «اعملُوا بالقرآن: أحلُّوا حَلاَلهُ، وحرَّمُوا حرامَهُ، وآمنوا بِه ولا تكفُروا بشيءٍ منهُ، وما اشتبه عليكُم فردُّوه إلي الله تعالى وإلى أولى العِلم من بَعدي، كيما يُخبرُونكم به» ، ثم قال: «وليَسعُكُم القرآن وما فيهِ من البَيَانِ فإنه شافعٌ مشفَّعٌ، وما حِلٌ مُصدَّقَّ (١) وأن له بكل حَرفٍ نُورًا يومَ القِيَاَمَة» . فردوا الأحكام إليه وإلى الرسول، إِنْ كُنْتُمْ تُؤْمِنُونَ بِاللَّهِ وَالْيَوْمِ الْآخِرِ فإن الإيمان يُوجِبُ ذلك. ذلِكَ الرد خَيْرٌ لكم وَأَحْسَنُ تَأْوِيلًا من تأويلكم بالرأي من غير رد، وأحسن عاقبة ومآلاً، والله تعالى أعلم. الإشارة: أولو الأمر عند الصوفية، هم شيوخ التربية العارفون بالله، فيجب على المريدين طاعتهم في المنشط والمكره، وفي كل ما أمروا به، فمن خالف أو قال: «لِمَ» لَم يفلح أبدًا، ويكفي الإشارة عن التصريح عند الحذاق أهل الاعتناء، فإن تعارض أمر الأمراء وأمر الشيوخ، قدَّم أمر الشيخ إلا لفتنة فادحة، فإن الشيخ يأمر بطاعتهم أيضًا لما يؤدي من الهرج بالفقراء، فإن تنازعتم يا معشر الفقراء، في شيء من علم الشريعة أو الطريقة، فردوه إلى الكتاب والسُّنة. قال الجنيد رضي الله عنه: طريقتنا هذه مؤيدة بالكتاب والسنة، فمن لم يقرأ القرآن ويتعلم الحديث لا يُقتدى به في هذا الشأن. هـ. ويكفي المهم من ذلك، وهو ما يتوقف عليه أمر عبادته. والله تعالى أعلم. ثم ذكر الحق تعالى من أعرض عن حكم الله ورسوله، ورضى بحكم غيرهما، فقال:

4:60–63 · 4:60–63 · Appeal to false judgment, repentance, and private admonition

Cairo I:520–522

“Have you not seen those who claim to believe in what has been sent down to you and what was sent down before you? They wish to take their disputes for judgment to the ṭāghūt , although they were commanded to reject it. Satan wishes to lead them far astray. When they are told, ‘Come to what God has sent down and to the Messenger,’ you see the hypocrites turning resolutely away from you. How, then, when a misfortune strikes them on account of what their own hands have sent ahead, and they come to you swearing by God, ‘We intended nothing but kindness and reconciliation’? Those are the people whose hearts God knows. Turn away from them, admonish them, and address to them words that penetrate their innermost selves.”

Language · Language and construction (*qultu*)

In “you see the hypocrites” the explicit noun replaces the pronoun one might have expected, “you see them”; it brands them with hypocrisy and censures them for it. Ṣudūdan (“turning away”) is either a verbal noun (maṣdar) or what the grammarians call an ism al-maṣdar, corresponding to ṣadd; Ibn ʿAjība distinguishes the verbal noun as naming the event or meaning and the ism al-maṣdar as naming its perceptible verbal expression. Yaḥlifūna (“swearing”) is circumstantial (ḥāl). The phrase fī anfusihim (“within themselves,” or “concerning their very selves”) is related syntactically to “say.” A proposed attachment to balīghan (“penetrating”) is weak, because a dependent of an attributive expression should not ordinarily precede it, except under a broader allowance for adverbial expressions.

Exposition · The outward interpretation (*yaqūlu l-Ḥaqq*)

God—exalted is His majesty—addresses Muḥammad: Have you not observed those hypocrites who merely claim faith in the revelation given to you and the books revealed before you? They nevertheless seek the judgment of the ṭāghūt: here Ibn ʿAjība identifies him as Kaʿb ibn al-Ashraf because of the magnitude of his transgression. The expression includes, by extension, everyone who judges according to falsehood. Yet these people were commanded to disown false authority, believe in God, and accept His judgment. Satan wishes to deflect them from the judgment of God and His Messenger into a remote error.

Ibn ʿAjība then relates an account attributed to Ibn ʿAbbās. A hypocrite and a Jewish man were in dispute. The Jewish litigant proposed that the Prophet decide their case, while the hypocrite proposed Kaʿb ibn al-Ashraf. They nevertheless went to the Messenger, who gave judgment in favour of the Jewish man, according to what was right. The hypocrite refused to accept it and proposed that they appeal to ʿUmar. The Jewish man agreed. On hearing that the Messenger had already judged and that the hypocrite rejected the decision, ʿUmar asked the latter whether it was so. When he answered yes, ʿUmar told them to wait, entered his house, took up his sword, and—according to this transmitted story—struck off the hypocrite's head, saying, “Thus do I judge one who will not accept God's and His Messenger's judgment.” The story associates the descent of the verses with this event and relates that Gabriel described ʿUmar as the one who distinguished truth from falsehood, whence the epithet al-Fārūq. [Translator’s note: This is an exegetical report, not an established ruling or an authorization of private violence.] [Translator’s note: Its transmission requires the separate historical scrutiny recorded in the notes.]

When they are summoned to God's revelation and the Messenger, some of these hypocrites avert themselves, refusing his judgment. What will become of them if misfortune strikes them—in the account just recited, the killing of their companion—as a consequence of rejecting the divine decision? They then come asking compensation for their companion, swearing that in going to ʿUmar they wanted only kindness to both litigants and reconciliation between them, so as to settle the dispute. But God knows the hypocrisy within their hearts; alternatively He knows their desire for blood compensation. Their concealment and false oaths cannot avail them before Him. Therefore the Messenger is told neither to accept their excuses nor permit them to obtain what they covet. He is to admonish them and speak to them privately, with words that reach the heart and restrain them from pursuing the claim. Privacy makes advice more effective and easier to accept. Hence the maxim: “Whoever counsels you when you are alone has counselled you; whoever counsels you before people has exposed you to shame.” God knows best.

Spiritual · (*al-ishāra*)

Whoever has placed himself under the spiritual authority (wilāya) of a shaykh of training (shaykh al-tarbiya) should, within that relationship, refer his disputes and affairs to him and accept his direction. Ibn ʿAjība criticizes certain fuqarāʾ who profess to be under a shaykh's training but then turn to the unjust rulers and judges of their time over worldly affairs and related matters. He says that they have strayed far astray, unless they repent and repair the harm by reconciling the shaykh's heart with them, so that he may mend their fracture. He says that the truthful aspirant will not go to a judge even if all his wealth is lost; if recourse is absolutely unavoidable, he should appoint a representative. [Translator’s note: This states Ibn ʿAjība's demanding counsel on discipleship, not a substitute for legal rights or a general rule barring access to justice.]

If such aspirants experience the calamity of a darkened heart and entanglement in worldly turmoil because they have crossed from their shaykh's ruling to another's, they may come insisting that they intended only to safeguard their property and reconcile with their opponent. The shaykh should refrain from reproach and instead remind them so that they repent. If they do repent, God is Forgiving, Merciful.

Transition ·

The command to obey the Messenger and seek his judgment in all affairs is repeated, combining warning with encouragement.

أَلَمْ تَرَ إِلَى الَّذِينَ يَزْعُمُونَ أَنَّهُمْ آمَنُوا بِما أُنْزِلَ إِلَيْكَ وَما أُنْزِلَ مِنْ قَبْلِكَ يُرِيدُونَ أَنْ يَتَحاكَمُوا إِلَى الطَّاغُوتِ وَقَدْ أُمِرُوا أَنْ يَكْفُرُوا بِهِ وَيُرِيدُ الشَّيْطانُ أَنْ يُضِلَّهُمْ ضَلالاً بَعِيداً (60) وَإِذا قِيلَ لَهُمْ تَعالَوْا إِلى ما أَنْزَلَ اللَّهُ وَإِلَى الرَّسُولِ رَأَيْتَ الْمُنافِقِينَ يَصُدُّونَ عَنْكَ صُدُوداً (61) فَكَيْفَ إِذا أَصابَتْهُمْ مُصِيبَةٌ بِما قَدَّمَتْ أَيْدِيهِمْ ثُمَّ جاؤُكَ يَحْلِفُونَ بِاللَّهِ إِنْ أَرَدْنا إِلاَّ إِحْساناً وَتَوْفِيقاً (62) أُولئِكَ الَّذِينَ يَعْلَمُ اللَّهُ ما فِي قُلُوبِهِمْ فَأَعْرِضْ عَنْهُمْ وَعِظْهُمْ وَقُلْ لَهُمْ فِي أَنْفُسِهِمْ قَوْلاً بَلِيغاً (63) قلت: (رأيت المنافقين) ، وضع الظاهر موضع المضمر تسجيلاً عليهم بالنفاق وذمًا لهم به. وكان القياس: رأيتهم، و (صدودًا) : مصدر، أو اسم مصدر الذي هو الصد، والفرق بينه وبين المصدر: أن المصدر اسم للمعنى الذي هو الحدث، واسم المصدر اسم للفظ المحسوس، و (يحلفون) حال. و (في أنفسهم) يتعلق بقُل، وقيل ببليغًا. وهو ضعيف لأن الصفات لا يتقدم عليها معمولها، اللهم إلا أن يتوسع في الظروف. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: أَلَمْ تَرَ يا محمد إِلَى الَّذِينَ يَزْعُمُونَ أَنَّهُمْ آمَنُوا بِما أُنْزِلَ إِلَيْكَ وَما أُنْزِلَ مِنْ قَبْلِكَ وهم المنافقون، يُرِيدُونَ أَنْ يَتَحاكَمُوا إِلَى الطَّاغُوتِ، كعب بن الأشرف لفرط طغيانه. وفي معناه كل من يحكم بالباطل، وَقَدْ أُمِرُوا أَنْ يَكْفُرُوا بِهِ، ويؤمنوا بالله ويرضوا بحكمه. وَيُرِيدُ الشَّيْطانُ أَنْ يُضِلَّهُمْ ضَلالًا بَعِيداً، بأن يصرفهم عن حكم الله ورسوله. قال ابن عباس: إنَّ منافقًا خَاصَمَ يَهُودِيًّا فَدَعَاهُ اليهوديّ إلى النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم، ودعاه المنافق إلى كعب بن الأشْرَف، ثم اختصما إلى رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم، فحكم لليهودي بالحق فلم يرض المنافق، وقال: نتحاكم إلى عُمَر، فقال اليهودي: نعم فذهبا إلى عمر رضي الله عنه فقال اليهودي: قضى لي رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم فلم يرض بقضائه وخاصم إليك. فقال عمر للمنافق: أكذلك؟. قال: نعم، فقال: على رسلكما حتى أخرج إليكما، فدخل وأخذ سيفه فخرج، فضرب به عنق المنافق حتى برد (١) ، وقال: هكذا أقضي لمن لم يرضَ بقضاء الله ورسوله، فنزلت الآية.. وقال جبريل عليه السلام: إن عمر فرّق بين الحق والباطل. فسُمي الفاروق. وَإِذا قِيلَ لَهُمْ تَعالَوْا إِلى ما أَنْزَلَ اللَّهُ وَإِلَى الرَّسُولِ رَأَيْتَ الْمُنافِقِينَ أي: بعضَهم، يَصُدُّونَ عَنْكَ غير راضين بحكمك صُدُوداً عظيمًا. فَكَيْفَ يكون حالهم إِذا أَصابَتْهُمْ مُصِيبَةٌ كقتل عمر المنافقَ، بسبب ما قدمت أَيْدِيهِمْ من عدم الرضى بحكم الله، ثُمَّ جاؤُكَ يطلبون ديّة صاحبهم، يَحْلِفُونَ بِاللَّهِ إِنْ أَرَدْنا بالانصراف إلى عمر إِلَّا إِحْساناً منه بالخصمين، وَتَوْفِيقاً بينهما، قطعًا للنزاع بينهما، قال تعالى: أُولئِكَ الَّذِينَ يَعْلَمُ اللَّهُ ما فِي قُلُوبِهِمْ من النفاق، فلا يغني عنهم الكتمان والحلف الكاذب من الله شيئًا، أو يعلم الله ما في قلوبهم من الطمع في الدية، فَأَعْرِضْ عَنْهُمْ، أي: عن قبول معذرتهم ولا تمكنهم من طمعهم، وَقُلْ لَهُمْ فِي أَنْفُسِهِمْ، أي: خاليًا بهم قَوْلًا بَلِيغاً يبلغ إلى قلوبهم، ويؤثر فيهم، لينزجروا عن طلب دم صاحبهم، وإنما أمر أن يعظهم خاليا بهم لأن النصح في ذلك أنجح، وأقرب للقبول، ولذلك قيل: من نصحك وَحدَكَ فقد نصحك، ومن نصحك مع الناس فقد فضحك. والله تعالى أعلم. الإشارة: كل من دخل تحت ولاية شيخ التربية، وجب أن يرد حكوماته كلها إليه، ويرضى بما قضى عليه، وترى بعض الفقراء يزعمون أنهم في تربية الشيخ وتحت أحكامه، ثم يتحاكمون إلى حُكام الجور وقضاة الزمان في أمر الدنيا وما يرجع إليها، فهؤلاء قد ضلوا ضلالاً بعيدًا. إلا أن يتوبوا ويُصلحوا ما أفسدوا، بإصلاح قلب الشيخ حتى يجبر كسرهم، فالمريد الصادق لا يصل إلى الحاكم، ولو ذهب ماله كله. فإن كان ولا بد. فليوكل عنه فى ذلك. فكيف إذا أصابت هؤلاء مصيبة وهي ظلمة القلب، وفتنة الدنيا بسبب ما قدمت أيديهم من تخطى حكم شيخهم إلى حُكم غيره، ثم جاؤوك يحلفون بالله ما أردنا إلا إحسانًا وهو حفظ مالنا، وتوفيقًا بيننا وبين خصمنا، فيجب على الشيخ أن يُعرض عن عتابهم ويذكرهم حتى يتوبوا،. فإن تابوا فإن الله غفور رحيم. ثم أعاد الأمر بطاعة الرسول وتحكيمه فى جميع الأمور ترهيبا وترغيبا، فقال:

4:64–68 · 4:64–68 · Prophetic judgment, inward surrender, and the fruit of obedience

Cairo I:522–524

“We have sent no messenger except that he should be obeyed, by God's leave. Had they, when they wronged themselves, come to you and asked God for forgiveness, and had the Messenger asked forgiveness for them, they would have found God Ever-Returning in forgiveness, Merciful. No—by your Lord! They do not believe until they make you judge in whatever has arisen between them, and then find no constriction within themselves concerning your judgment, but surrender in complete surrender. Had We prescribed for them, ‘Kill yourselves’ or ‘Leave your homes,’ none but a few of them would have done it. Had they done what they were admonished to do, it would have been better for them and more firmly established them. Then We would have given them from Our presence a tremendous reward and guided them upon a straight path.”

Language ·

If wajada (“find”) is treated as a verb of cognition, the sense is “they would have known God to be accepting of repentance and merciful.” [Grammatical and textual note: On this analysis Allāh is the first object, with acceptance of repentance and mercy predicated of Him. Cairo I:522 instead calls tawwāban and raḥīman “the two objects” of wajada, a faulty formula because it leaves the already expressed first object, Allāh, unaccounted for. The explanatory English follows the contrast made explicit by al-Bayḍāwī and al-ʿUkbarī on 4:64; it does not claim to reproduce a corrected Arabic witness.] If wajada means to encounter or come upon, tawwāban is circumstantial, while raḥīman is either substitutive (badal) or another circumstance tied to the relevant pronoun.

Exposition ·

From Adam's day to Muḥammad's, God has sent no messenger but that the messenger should be obeyed by His leave and command. Ibn ʿAjība says that one who rejects a messenger's authority and refuses his judgments thereby rejects him. Had the hypocrites come repentantly to the Messenger after wronging themselves by seeking another tribunal and appealing to the ṭāghūt, and had they asked God's pardon while the Messenger sought forgiveness on their behalf, they would have experienced God's readiness to receive repentance and His mercy. The verse speaks of “the Messenger” rather than simply “you”—“had you asked forgiveness for them”—to magnify his station, and to indicate that he should receive the apology of sincere penitents, however grave their offence, and intercede for them. That he may intercede in matters of the greatest sins belongs to the dignity of his office.

God then swears by His Lordship that those who refuse the Messenger's judgment do not possess faith in its true sense. They must bring their disputes to him, willingly accept what he rules, and experience neither inward narrowness nor doubt about it; rather their breasts should expand at his decision, for it is truth from God. Their surrender must be outward and inward together.

If they had been charged to kill themselves by way of repentance, as the children of Israel were charged in the episode invoked here—or to risk their lives in striving in God's way—or ordered to abandon their homes, as Israel was ordered to migrate from Egypt, only a few, the sincerely devoted, would have complied. Abū Bakr is reported to have said that had emigration been enjoined he would have been the first to leave. Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās is said to have declared that if the Messenger ordered him to kill himself, he would obey. Similar expressions of resolve are attributed to ʿUmar, ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir, Ibn Masʿūd, and other Companions. When this reached the Prophet, the account reports him saying that some men of his community have faith in their hearts firmer than immovable mountains. Ibn ʿAjība counts them among the “few.” [Translator’s note: These extraordinary hypothetical declarations belong to the account's rhetorical illustration of obedience; they are not instructions addressed to the reader.]

Concerning the occasion of verse 65, he recounts the dispute of al-Zubayr with Ḥāṭib over irrigation channels in the stony ground (shirāj al-ḥarra). They watered date palms from the same supply. The Messenger first said, “Water, Zubayr, then release [the water] to your neighbour.” The neighbour retorted that this was because al-Zubayr was the son of the Messenger's paternal aunt. The Prophet then told al-Zubayr to water fully, holding back the flow until it reached the boundary banks (judur), and thus to take his complete due. Another report links the verse to the Jewish litigant and hypocrite discussed above, which Ibn ʿAjība judges more fitting to the immediate sequence. [Translator’s note: The irrigation report’s sound core and Ibn ʿAjība’s identification of its other litigant are distinct questions.]

If the people had practised the exhortation to obey the Messenger and accept his judgment, it would have been better for their affairs in this world and the next, firmer for their religion and stronger for their faith; alternatively, it would have secured the reward for their works. In that event God would bestow immense reward from His own presence and guide them on the straight road, leading them to the holy Presence and continual intimacy, opening to them the mysteries of the sciences and treasuries of understanding. Ibn ʿAjība cites the saying ascribed to the Prophet, “Whoever acts according to what he knows, God bequeaths to him knowledge of what he did not know.” [Source note: Abū Nuʿaym, Ḥilyat al-awliyāʾ X:14–15, explains that a saying transmitted from Jesus through a Successor was mistakenly attributed to the Prophet and furnished with a chain. He rejects that chain as a basis for attributing it through Aḥmad b. Ḥanbal. This criticism concerns that attribution and chain; it does not decide the maxim’s use by Sufi teachers.] God knows best.

Spiritual ·

Just as God commanded obedience to His Messenger during his lifetime, He has commanded obedience after the Prophet's passing to his heirs: God-fearing scholars who exercise justice in judgments and the saints who know God and judge through inspired understanding (waḥy al-ilhām). The scholars judge for the community at large; the saints judge concerning their particular disciples and those who voluntarily attach themselves to their training. Whoever resists the just decision of learned authorities and feels inward constriction has a trait of hypocrisy. Whoever rejects the direction of the saints withdraws from their circle and from the sphere of their training. For, Ibn ʿAjība says, the judgment of the Messenger and that of his heirs is God's judgment; whoever does not accept God's judgment has departed from the circle of faith.

Faith reaches perfection only when a servant ceases to find constriction in the workings of God's determinations, whether irresistible (qahriyya) or prescriptive (taklīfiyya), and submits to whatever manifests from eternal power: poverty or wealth, humiliation or honour, withholding or gift, contraction (qabḍ) or expansion (basṭ), illness or health, and the remaining variations of destiny. To accept them both outwardly and inwardly is to surrender the management (tadbīr) of one's affairs and one's self-directed choosing (ikhtiyār) to the choice of one's Master, who knows the servant's true good more fully and is more merciful to him than his mother or father. God grants success and guides to the even road.

Transition ·

God next promises the obedient a splendid reward to encourage them in obedience to the Messenger.

وَما أَرْسَلْنا مِنْ رَسُولٍ إِلاَّ لِيُطاعَ بِإِذْنِ اللَّهِ وَلَوْ أَنَّهُمْ إِذْ ظَلَمُوا أَنْفُسَهُمْ جاؤُكَ فَاسْتَغْفَرُوا اللَّهَ وَاسْتَغْفَرَ لَهُمُ الرَّسُولُ لَوَجَدُوا اللَّهَ تَوَّاباً رَحِيماً (64) فَلا وَرَبِّكَ لا يُؤْمِنُونَ حَتَّى يُحَكِّمُوكَ فِيما شَجَرَ بَيْنَهُمْ ثُمَّ لا يَجِدُوا فِي أَنْفُسِهِمْ حَرَجاً مِمَّا قَضَيْتَ وَيُسَلِّمُوا تَسْلِيماً (65) وَلَوْ أَنَّا كَتَبْنا عَلَيْهِمْ أَنِ اقْتُلُوا أَنْفُسَكُمْ أَوِ اخْرُجُوا مِنْ دِيارِكُمْ ما فَعَلُوهُ إِلاَّ قَلِيلٌ مِنْهُمْ وَلَوْ أَنَّهُمْ فَعَلُوا ما يُوعَظُونَ بِهِ لَكانَ خَيْراً لَهُمْ وَأَشَدَّ تَثْبِيتاً (66) وَإِذاً لَآتَيْناهُمْ مِنْ لَدُنَّا أَجْراً عَظِيماً (67) وَلَهَدَيْناهُمْ صِراطاً مُسْتَقِيماً (68) قلت: (توابًا رحيمًا) مفعولا (وَجَدَ) إن كانت علمية، أو (توابًا) حال، و (رحيمًا) بدل منه، أو حال من ضميره إن فسرت بصادف. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: وَما أَرْسَلْنا مِنْ رَسُولٍ من لدن آدم إلى زمانك، إِلَّا لِيُطاعَ بِإِذْنِ اللَّهِ وأمره بطاعته، فمن لم يطعه ولم يرض بأحكامه فهو كافر به. وَلَوْ أَنَّهُمْ أي: المنافقون حين ظَلَمُوا أَنْفُسَهُمْ بالترافع إلى غيرك، والتحاكم إلى الطاغوت جاؤُكَ تائبين فَاسْتَغْفَرُوا اللَّهَ بالتوبة، وَاسْتَغْفَرَ لَهُمُ الرَّسُولُ حين اعتذروا إليه حتى انتصب لهم شفيعًا، لَوَجَدُوا اللَّهَ أي: تحققوا كونه تَوَّاباً رَحِيماً، قابلاً لتوبتهم متفضلاً عليهم بالرحمة والغفران. وإنما عدل عن الخطاب في قوله: وَاسْتَغْفَرَ لَهُمُ الرَّسُولُ ولم يقل: واستغفرت لهم، تفخيمًا لشأنه، وتنبيهًا على أن من حق الرسول أن يقبل اعتذار التائبين، وإن عَظُم جُرمُهم، ويشفع لهم، ومن جلالة منصبه أن يشفع في عظائم الذنوب وكبائرها. ثم أقسم بربوبيته على نفي إيمان من لم يرض بحكم رسوله، فقال: فَلا وَرَبِّكَ لا يُؤْمِنُونَ إيمانًا حقيقيًا حَتَّى يُحَكِّمُوكَ أي: يترافعوا إليك، راضين بحكمك، فِيما شَجَرَ بَيْنَهُمْ أي: اختلط بينهم واختلفوا فيه ثُمَّ لا يَجِدُوا فِي أَنْفُسِهِمْ حَرَجاً أي: ضيقًا وشكًا مِمَّا قَضَيْتَ، بل تنشرح صدورهم لحكمك لأنه حق من عند الله. وَيُسَلِّمُوا لأمرك تَسْلِيماً. أي: ينقادوا لأمرك ظاهرًا وباطنًا. وَلَوْ أَنَّا كَتَبْنا عَلَيْهِمْ أَنِ اقْتُلُوا أَنْفُسَكُمْ، توبة من ذنوبكم، كما كتبناه على بني إسرائيل، أو في الجهاد في سبيل الله، أَوِ اخْرُجُوا مِنْ دِيارِكُمْ كما خرج بنو إسرائيل حين أمرناهم بالهجرة من مصر، ما فَعَلُوهُ إِلَّا قَلِيلٌ مِنْهُمْ وهم المخلصون. قال أبو بكر الصديق رضي الله عنه: (لو كتب ذلك علينا لكنت أنا أول خارج) . قال ثابت بن قيس بن شماس: (لو أمرني رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم أن أقتل نفسي لفعلت) . وكذلك قال عُمر وعمارُ بن ياسر وابنُ مسعود وناس من أصحاب رسول الله صلّى الله عليه وسلم: لو أمرنا لفعلنا. فبلغ ذلك النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم فقال: «إنَّ مِن أُمَّتِي رِجَالاً: الإيمَانُ في قُلُوبِهِم أثبَتُ مِنَ الجِبَالِ الرَّوَاسِي» . فهؤلاء من القليل. وسبب نزول قوله: فَلا وَرَبِّكَ.. الخ: قضية الزُّبَيرِ مع حَاطِب في شرَاج الحَرَّة (١) ، كَانَا يسقيانِ به النّخل، فتخاصما إلى رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم فقال عليه السلام: «اسقِ يا زُبيرُ وأرسِل إلى جارِكَ» ، فقال حَاطبُ: لأن كَان ابن عمتك. فقال- عليه الصلاة والسلام-: «اسقِ يا زُبيرُ، واحِبِس الماءَ حتَّى يبلغ الجدر (٢) واستوف حقك» . وقيل: نزلت في اليهودي مع المنافق المتقدم، وهو أليق بالسياق. وَلَوْ أَنَّهُمْ فَعَلُوا ما يُوعَظُونَ بِهِ من طاعة الرسول، والرضى بحكمه، لَكانَ خَيْراً لَهُمْ في آجلهم وعاجلهم، وَأَشَدَّ تَثْبِيتاً في دينهم وقوة في إيمانهم، أو تثبيتًا لثواب أعمالهم، وَإِذاً لو فعلوا ذلك لَآتَيْناهُمْ مِنْ لَدُنَّا أَجْراً عَظِيماً وَلَهَدَيْناهُمْ صِراطاً مُسْتَقِيماً يصلون بسلوكه إلى حضرة القدس، ودوام الأنس، ويفتح لهم أسرار العلوم، ومخازن الفهوم، قال صلّى الله عليه وسلم: «من عَمِلَ بِمَا عَلِمَ أَوْرَثَهُ اللهُ علمَ مَا لم يعلم» . والله تعالى أعلم. الإشارة: كما أمر الله بطاعة رسوله صلّى الله عليه وسلم في حياته، أمر بطاعة ورثته بعد مماته، وهم العلماء الأتقياء الذين يعدلون في الأحكام، والأولياء العارفون الذين يحكمون بوحي الإلهام، فالعلماء حُكَّام على العموم، والأولياء حكام على الخصوص، أعني من تعلق بهم من أهل الإرادة، فمن لم يرض بحكم العلماء، ووجد في نفسه حرجًا مما قضوا به عليه، ففيه شعبة من النفاق، وخصلة من المنافقين. ومن لم يرض بحكم الأولياء فقد خرج من دائرتهم، ومن عُش تربيتهم، لأن حكم الرسول- عليه الصلاة والسلام- وحكم ورثته هو حكم الله، ومن لم يرض بحكم الله خرج عن دائرة الإيمان. فلا يَكمُلُ إيمَانُ العَبدِ حتَّى لا يجد في نفسه حرجًا من أحكام الله، القهرية والتكليفية، ويسلم لما يبرز من عنصر القدرة الأزلية، كيفما كان، فقرًا أو غنى، ذلاً أو عزًا، منعًا أو عطاء، قبضًا أو بسطًا، مرضًا أو صحة، إلى غير ذلك من اختلاف المقادير. ويرضى بذلك ظاهرَا وباطنًا، وينسلخ من تدبيره واختياره إلى اختيار مولاه فهو أعلم بمصالحه، وأرحم به من أمه وأبيه: وبالله التوفيق. وهو الهادي إلى سواء الطريق. ثم وعد المطيعين وعدا جميلا، وخيرا جزيلا، ترغيبا فى امتثال ما أُمر به من طاعة الرسول، فقال:

4:69–70 · 4:69–70 · Prophets, truthful ones, martyrs, and the degrees of companionship

Cairo I:524–526

“Whoever obeys God and the Messenger—those shall be with the ones upon whom God has bestowed favour: the prophets, the utterly truthful, the martyrs, and the righteous. How excellent are they as companions! Such is the favour of God, and God suffices as Knower.”

Language ·

Rafīqan (“as companion”) is a specification (tamyīz) explaining the commendatory or exclamatory force of ḥasuna. The singular is used with reference to several people because the adjective faʿīl may be applied to singular and plural alike; alternatively the intention is to commend each of them individually.

Exposition ·

Those who obey God and the Messenger, accept their judgments, carry out their commands and shun their prohibitions, will be among God's most honoured creatures, those upon whom He has bestowed grace. These are the prophets and messengers; the ṣiddīqūn, whose truthfulness, affirmation of truth and certainty have become great—identified here with the saints who know God; the martyrs (shuhadāʾ) who have died fighting in God's path; and the righteous (ṣāliḥūn), including devout scholars and the upright among Muslims generally.

Al-Bayḍāwī, whom Ibn ʿAjība quotes at length, divides them into four groups in correspondence with their stations of knowledge and action, urging everyone not to lag behind. The prophets possess perfection in both knowledge and practice, and pass beyond the attainment of perfection in themselves to perfecting others. Next come the ṣiddīqūn, whose souls rise toward the summit of knowing: at one time ascending by examination of arguments and signs, at another by purification and spiritual exercises, until they perceive things and give account of them as they are. Then come the martyrs, whose ardour for obedience and striving to manifest the truth leads them to offer their very lives to exalt God's word. Finally come the righteous, who devote their years to His obedience and their circumstances to what pleases Him. [Translator’s source note: Bayḍāwī’s Anwār al-tanzīl, II:83, under Q4:69, reads وأموالهم في مرضاته, “and their wealth to what pleases Him.” Cairo instead prints وأحوالهم, “and their circumstances,” retained above. The cited source therefore differs in meaning at this word; it does not by itself establish Ibn ʿAjība’s intended wording.]

Al-Bayḍāwī offers another classification: those favoured are people who know God, and their knowing occurs either by reaching immediate vision (ʿiyān) or while remaining in inference and demonstration (istidlāl and burhān). Those who behold directly may have, in addition, an intimate nearness analogous to seeing something close at hand: these are the prophets. Or they may be like a person who sees from a distance: these are the ṣiddīqūn. Those who remain with proofs may know through decisive demonstrations, and thus are deeply rooted scholars, God's witnesses on earth; or through indications and persuasive reasons in which their souls rest, and thus are the righteous. Here ends the cited discourse.

Ibn ʿAjība raises two objections. First, al-Bayḍāwī applies the name ʿārif—one possessing realized knowledge—to those who have knowledge through inference, while Sufis reserve it for those who pass beyond mere inference; otherwise the person is an ʿālim, a scholar. Second, he locates the ṣiddīqūn among those seeing from afar. But, says Ibn ʿAjība, people of self-effacement (fanāʾ) no longer experience remoteness: they have become lost in nearness, to the point that their name and trace are effaced. What distance or separation can remain to the knower? He remarks that this mistake comes from lacking direct taste (dhawq), and each discipline has its own masters. He promises further clarification in the ishāra.

“How excellent are these companions” in the lofty paradises! Those who accompany them enjoy seeing, visiting and being present with them even when the companions occupy higher degrees. Sharing company need not mean equality of rank. The marginal commentary adds that even one below a prophet's station may have a sort of companionship through nearness to his inward state and unveiling without being veiled from him, though he cannot aspire to the prophet's own degree. It adduces the illumination, intimacy and profit obtained by others when they inwardly perceive him. Ibn ʿAjība transmits a report that those of the higher ranks in Paradise visit the lower, but not the reverse, except that one who visited another for God's sake in this life may visit where he wishes in Paradise. The transmission of this specific report remains to be examined.

He also narrates that Thawbān, the Prophet's freedman, came to him one day with changed countenance and a wasted body. Asked what was wrong, he replied that he was not physically ill: when he could not see the Prophet he felt an overwhelming longing and estrangement until they met again. He then thought of the hereafter and feared that, while the Prophet would be elevated among the prophets, he himself would either enter Paradise on a lower level or not enter it at all, and so might never see him again. The verse “Whoever obeys God and the Messenger…” was then revealed, according to the report.

“This is the favour of God”: the expression includes the recompense promised to the obedient and the further gifts of nearness (qurb) and presence (ḥuḍūr). It is bestowed freely by God. He knows the degrees of works and stations and recompenses each according to his place. God knows best.

Spiritual ·

Outward, bodily obedience brings sensory companionship in the bodily bliss of Paradise; inward obedience of the heart brings spiritual companionship in spiritual felicity. The one whose companionship remains merely sensory experiences separation (farq), while the possessor of inward companionship is gathered in unity (jamʿ) and never absent from the Beloved for a moment. These are the ṣiddīqūn, the brought-near; above them stand the prophets, below them the martyrs and righteous.

Knowledge of God arises either through the removal of the veil and dispersal of the cloud—the cloud of created effects (athar)—and these are the people of direct witnessing (shuhūd) and vision (ʿiyān); or from behind the veil, where seekers receive their reward beyond the doorway, proceeding from effects to the One who acts (muʾaththir) through evidence and demonstration. Those of the first class may attain the direct address of revelation and sight of the noble angels: these are the prophets and messengers. Others stop short of prophetic revelation, yet receive inspiration (ilhām): these are the ṣiddīqūn, people of spiritual states and established stations (aḥwāl and maqāmāt). Both share the station of direct vision, but the expanse of the Presence is immeasurable, and ascent through the mysteries of divine unity has no end. Where the saint's foot comes to rest, the prophet's ascent begins.

Those behind the veil know God either through decisive demonstrations and scriptural evidences: these are the rooted scholars, corresponding in this inner classification to the station of witnesses (shuhadāʾ); or through discipline (riyāḍa), struggle (mujāhada) and repeated extraordinary graces (karāmāt): these are the worshippers and renunciants, the station of the righteous. Ordinary Muslims are joined to the latter class, for within each named station are innumerable degrees and lesser stations known fully only to the One who knows them. God knows best.

Transition ·

The discourse now encourages striving in God's way, the immediate subject toward which the preceding directions have been leading.

وَمَنْ يُطِعِ اللَّهَ وَالرَّسُولَ فَأُولئِكَ مَعَ الَّذِينَ أَنْعَمَ اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِمْ مِنَ النَّبِيِّينَ وَالصِّدِّيقِينَ وَالشُّهَداءِ وَالصَّالِحِينَ وَحَسُنَ أُولئِكَ رَفِيقاً (69) ذلِكَ الْفَضْلُ مِنَ اللَّهِ وَكَفى بِاللَّهِ عَلِيماً (70) قلت: «رفيقاً» : تمييز لما في (حَسُن) من معنى التعجب أو المدح، ولم يجمع لأن فعيلا يُحمل على الواحد والجمع، أو لأنه أريد حسن كل واحد منهم. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: وَمَنْ يُطِعِ اللَّهَ وَالرَّسُولَ ويرضى بأحكامهما ويمتثل أمرهما ويجتنب نهيهما، فَأُولئِكَ مَعَ الَّذِينَ أَنْعَمَ اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِمْ، وهم أكرم الخلق عند الله وأعظمهم قدرًا مِنَ النَّبِيِّينَ والمرسلين وَالصِّدِّيقِينَ وهم من كثر صدقهم وتصديقهم وعظم يقينهم وهم الأولياء العارفون بالله، وَالشُّهَداءِ الذين ماتوا جهادًا في سبيل الله، وَالصَّالِحِينَ وهم العلماء الأتقياء، ومن صلح حاله من عامة المسلمين. قال البيضاوي: قسمهم أربعة أقسام، بحسب منازلهم في العلم والعمل، وحث كافة الناس على ألاَّ يتأخروا عنهم. وهم: الأنبياء الفائزون بكمال العلم والعمل، المتجاوزون حد الكمال إلى درجة التكميل. ثم الصديقون الذين صعِدت نفوسهم تارة بمراقي النظر في الحجج والآيات، وأخرى بمعارج التصفية والرياضات إلى أوج العرفان، حتى اطلعوا على الأشياء وأخبروا عنها على ما هي عليها. ثم الشهداء الذين أدى بهم الحرص على الطاعة والجد في إظهار الحق، حتى بذلوا مُهَجَهُم في إعلاء كلمة الله، ثم الصالحون الذين صرفوا أعمارهم في طاعته، وأحوالهم في مرضاته. ولك أن تقول: المُنعَمُ عليهم هم العارفون بالله، وهؤلاء إما أن يكونوا بالغين درجة العيان، أو واقفين في مقام الاستدلال والبرهان، والأولون إما أن ينالوا مع العيان القرب، بحيث يكونون كمن يرى الشيء قريبًا، وهم الأنبياء، أو لا، فيكونون كمن يرى الشيء بعيدًا، وهم الصديقون، والآخرون إما أن يكون عرفانهم بالبراهين القاطعة، وهم العلماء الراسخون الذين هم شهداء الله في أرضه. وإما أن يكون بأمارات وإقناعات تطمئن إليها نفوسهم، وهم الصالحون. انتهى كلامه. وفيه نظر من وجهين: أحدهما: أنه أطلق على أهل الاستدلال أنهم عارفون، ولا يقال عند الصوفية فيه عارف، حتى يترقى عن مقام الاستدلال، وإلا فهو عالم فقط، والثاني: أنه جعل الصديقين بمنزلة من يرى الشيء بعيدًا، وأهل الفناء لم يبق لهم بُعدٌ، بل غابوا في القرب حتى امتحى اسمهم ورسمهم. فأيُّ بينونة وأيُّ بُعد يبقى للعارف؟ لولا فقدان الذوق، ولكن لكلِّ فنِ أربابُه، وسيأتي في الإشارة تحقيق ذلك إن شاء الله. ثم قال جلّ جلاله: وَحَسُنَ أُولئِكَ رَفِيقاً أي: ما أحسنهم رفقًا في الفراديس العُلى، فهم يتمتعون فيها برؤيتهم وزيارتهم والحضور معهم، وإن كانوا أعلى منهم، فلا يلزم من كونه معهم أن تستوي درجته معهم، قال في الحاشية: وتعقل مرافقة من دون النبي في المدانات من حاله وكشفه، بحيث لا يحجب عنه، وإن كان لا مطمع له في منزلته، واعتبر برؤية البصائر له وعدم غيبته عنهم وأنسهم به والاستفادة منه، وروى عنه- عليه الصلاة والسلام- أنه قال: «يزور الأعلَون من أهل الجنة الأسفلين، ولا يزور الأسفلون الأعلين، إلا من كان يزور في الله في الدنيا، فذلك يزور فى الجنة حيث شاء» . رُوِيَ أن ثَوبَانَ مَولى رَسُول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم أتاه يومًا وَقَد تَغَيَّر وَجهُهُ وَنَحَلَ جِسمُهُ، فسأله عليه الصلاة والسلام عن حاله، فقال: ما بي وَجَعٌ، غَيرَ أنِّي إذَا لَمْ أرَكَ اشتقْتُ إليك، واستَوحَشتُ وَحشةً شَدِيدَةً حَتَى ألقَاكَ، ثمَّ ذَكَرتُ الآخِرَةَ فخفت ألا أرَاكَ هُنَاكَ لأني عرفت أنكَ تُرفَعُ مَعَ النَّبِيِّينَ. وإن دخلتُ الجَنَّة، كُنتُ في مَنزلٍ أدون مِن مَنزِلكَ، وإن لَم أدخُلِ الجَنَّةَ فّذَلكَ حَرِيّ ألا أرَاكَ أبَدًا. فنزلت الآية وَمَنْ يُطِعِ اللَّهَ وَالرَّسُولَ ... الخ. ذلِكَ الْفَضْلُ مِنَ اللَّهِ إشارة إلى ما للمطيعين من الأجور، ومزيد القرب والحضور، وأنه فضل تفضل على عباده، وَكَفى بِاللَّهِ عَلِيماً بمقادير الأعمال والمقامات، فيُجازى كُلاًّ على حسب مقامه. والله تعالى أعلم. الإشارة: اعلم أن الطاعة التي توجب المعية الحسية في النعيم الحسي الجسماني هي الطاعة الظاهرة الحسية. والطاعة التي توجب المعية المعنوية في النعيم الروحاني هي الطاعة الباطنية القلبية. فالمعية الحسية صاحبها مفروق، والمعية المعنوية صاحبها مجموع، لا يغيب عن حبيبه لحظة. هؤلاء هم الصديقون المقربون. وفوقهم الأنبياء، وتحتهم الشهداء والصالحون. وبيان ذلك أن العلم بالله تعالى: إما أن يكون عن كشف الحجاب وانقشاع السحاب، أعني سحاب الأثر، وهم أهل الشهود والعيان. وإما أن يكون من وراء الحجاب، يأخذون أجرهم من وراء الباب، يستدلون بالآثار على المؤثر. وهم أهل الدليل والبرهان. والأولون إما أن يرتقوا إلى مكافحة الوحي ورؤية الملائكة الكرام. وهم الأنبياء والرسل- عليهم الصلاة والسلام-، وإما أن يقصروا عن درجة الوحي ويكون لهم وحي إلهام، وهم الصديقون أهل الحال والمقام، فقد اشتركوا في مقام العيانِ. لكن مقام الحضرة فضاؤه واسع، والترقي في معارج أسرار التوحيد غير متناهٍ، فحيث انتهى قدم الولي ابتدأ ترقي النبي، وأما أهل الحجاب فإما أن يكون علمهم بالله بالبراهين القطعية والدلائل السمعية، وهم العلماء الراسخون، وهو مقام الشهداء، وإما أن يكون علمهم بالرياضات والمجاهدات وتواتر الكرامات، وهم العباد والزهاد. وهو مقام الصالحين، ويلتحق بهم عوام المسلمين، لأن كل مقام من هذه المقامات فيه درجات ومقامات لا يحصرها إلا العالم بها. والله تعالى أعلم. ثم رغّب في الجهاد الذي هو المقصود، فقال:

4:71–74 · 4:71–74 · Vigilance, the reluctant self, and the struggle for sincerity

Cairo I:526–528

“O believers, take your precautions: go forth in detachments or go forth all together. Among you there is certainly one who hangs back; if a calamity befalls you, he says, ‘God has been gracious to me, for I was not there with them to witness it.’ But if favour from God reaches you, he will surely say—as though there had been no affection between you and him—‘Would that I had been with them, so that I might have won a mighty success!’ Let those who sell the life of this world for the hereafter fight in God's way. Whoever fights in God's way and is killed or prevails, We shall give him a tremendous reward.”

Language · Language (*qultu*)

The words ḥidhr and ḥadhar (“caution”) are variants, like shibh and shabah. The verb baṭuʾa is intransitive, “to be slow, heavy”; the doubled form baṭṭaʾa is transitive, “to delay another.” In la-man la-yubaṭṭiʾanna the first lām emphasizes the opening of the statement, while the second indicates an oath: “Among you—by God, I swear—is one who will certainly cause delay.” The phrase ka-an lam yakun baynakum wa-baynahu mawadda (“as though there had never been any affection between you and him”) is parenthetic between the verb “he will say” and the saying reported, drawing attention to the shallowness of his attachment to the believers.

Exposition ·

God tells believers to prepare themselves for the struggle against their enemies, taking every precaution of equipment and numbers rather than casting themselves to destruction. The text, says Ibn ʿAjība, offers no proof for the view he attributes to the Qadarites, because precaution is one of the causes with which God veils the mysteries of divine power. The Prophet himself was told that nothing would befall the believers except what God had decreed, and the saying “Tie it and place your trust [in God]” brings tranquillity to hearts not yet at rest and gives a practical ordinance to those whose faith is weak. Having prepared, believers may go forth in separate units, expedition following expedition (thubāt), or together with the Prophet or their commander.

Among those outwardly counted with Muslims, however, were the hypocrite ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy and others of his kind. Such a person either holds others back from fighting or becomes sluggish and declines to go. If killing or defeat befalls the believers, he rejoices that God spared him by keeping him away from the battle. If they receive victory and spoils, he regrets not being there, wishing for the wealth and worldly honour they gained. Despite the affection he professes, his conduct is that of an estranged enemy: he waits for calamities to strike them, delights in their adversity, and grieves at their success.

If such a person hangs back or hinders others, the people of genuine faith and sincerity should be the ones to struggle in God's way. They “sell the life of this world for the hereafter,” preferring what endures to what passes away. Whoever contends to make God's word uppermost, whether he is killed as a martyr or God grants him victory, receives a mighty reward. Ibn ʿAjība, following al-Bayḍāwī, draws attention to the wording “is killed or prevails”: the participant must remain steadfast, honouring himself through martyrdom if it occurs, or honouring the religion through victory. His immediate purpose should not be his own death, but the manifestation of the truth and the honour of the religion.

Spiritual ·

“O you who believe with the faith of the elect, take precautions against the deceits of your lower souls (nufūs), lest they prevent you from reaching the holy Presence. Go forth against them in detachments or together.” He adduces the saying that God's hand is with the community, emphasizing the condition of companionship (ṣuḥba) in Sufi training. The people of the path say that no one attains success without keeping company with someone who has attained it. A living ego-self does not die while living among ego-selves; it dies in the company of those who are themselves dead to selfishness. A fish remains alive while still in the water with other fish, but if removed from its element it quickly dies. Ibn ʿAjība says this image came from his own shaykh.

Within each of you is a self that delays departure toward the sanctity of the Presence. It flees places of hardship and trial, although there, if it understood, lies its true life. When members of the path meet a reversal or a severe manifestation of divine dealing (taʿarruf), while that self has escaped it, it congratulates itself on having been spared. If later the people of the path receive divine breaths (nafaḥāt rabbāniyya) and the wine-like delights of eternity, it wishes it had joined them and attained their success. Whoever desires victory in reaching the Presence should set his soul to endure what it dislikes and withhold from it the desires it finds easy. He proceeds in this struggle until he dies or conquers the ego and so obtains mastery over it. A shaykh said that the course of wayfarers ends in victory over their lower selves: when they conquer those selves, they arrive. Burdens and afflictions then depart, and every season becomes a festival.

Ibn ʿAjība cites verses:

Time becomes obedient to you and humankind your servants;
live each day of your days as a feast.

And further poetry:

A secret has appeared to you whose concealment from you endured so long;
a dawn has gleamed whose darkness you yourself had been.

You are the veil upon the heart, concealing the mystery of its unseen;
but for you, its seal would never have been impressed upon it.

When you vanish from the heart, the secret settles in it,
and its tents are pitched above the guarded procession of unveiling.

Then comes a discourse never wearisome to hear,
lovely to us in its prose and its measured verse.

When the soul hears it, its delight becomes sweet,
and the afflicted heart is relieved of its consuming passion.

Transition ·

God next reproves the servants for not rising to the defence of the weak.

يا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا خُذُوا حِذْرَكُمْ فَانْفِرُوا ثُباتٍ أَوِ انْفِرُوا جَمِيعاً (71) وَإِنَّ مِنْكُمْ لَمَنْ لَيُبَطِّئَنَّ فَإِنْ أَصابَتْكُمْ مُصِيبَةٌ قالَ قَدْ أَنْعَمَ اللَّهُ عَلَيَّ إِذْ لَمْ أَكُنْ مَعَهُمْ شَهِيداً (72) وَلَئِنْ أَصابَكُمْ فَضْلٌ مِنَ اللَّهِ لَيَقُولَنَّ كَأَنْ لَمْ تَكُنْ بَيْنَكُمْ وَبَيْنَهُ مَوَدَّةٌ يا لَيْتَنِي كُنْتُ مَعَهُمْ فَأَفُوزَ فَوْزاً عَظِيماً (73) فَلْيُقاتِلْ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ الَّذِينَ يَشْرُونَ الْحَياةَ الدُّنْيا بِالْآخِرَةِ وَمَنْ يُقاتِلْ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ فَيُقْتَلْ أَوْ يَغْلِبْ فَسَوْفَ نُؤْتِيهِ أَجْراً عَظِيماً (74) قلت: الحِذر والحَذَر واحد، كالشِّبه والشِّبَه، وبَطُأ يستعمل لازمًا بمعنى ثقل، ومتعديًا- بالتضعيف- أي: بطَّأ غيره، و (لَمَن ليبطئن) اللام الأولى للابتداء، والثانية للقسم، أي: وإنَّ منكم- أُقسم بالله- لمن ليبطئن. وجملة: (كأن لم يكن) : اعتراضية بين القول والمقول، تنبيهًا على ضعف عقيدتهم، وأن قولهم هذا قولُ من لا مواصلة بينكم وبينه. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: يا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا تأهبوا واستعدوا لجهاد الأعداء، وخُذُوا حِذْرَكُمْ منهم بالعُدَّةِ والعَدَد، وَلاَ تُلْقُواْ بِأَيْدِيكُمْ إِلَى التهلكة، ولا حجة فيه للقدرية لأن هذا من الأسباب التي ستر الله بها أسرار القدرة. وقد قال لنبيّه- عليه الصلاة والسلام-: قُلْ لَنْ يُصِيبَنا إِلَّا ما كَتَبَ اللَّهُ لَنا وقال- عليه الصلاة السلام-: «اعقلها وتوكل» . وفي ذلك طمأنينة للقلوب التي لم تطمئن وتشريعًا للضعفاء، فإذا تأهبتم واستعددتم فَانْفِرُوا أي: اخرجوا إلى الجهاد ثُباتٍ أي: جماعات متفرقة، سرية بعد سرية، أَوِ انْفِرُوا جَمِيعاً أي: مجتمعين مع نبيكم، أو مع أميركم. وَإِنَّ مِنْكُمْ يا معشر المسلمين لَمَنْ لَيُبَطِّئَنَّ الناس عن الجهاد، أو ليتثاقلن ويتخلفن عنه، وهو عبد الله بن أبي المنافق، وأشباهه من المنافقين، فَإِنْ أَصابَتْكُمْ مُصِيبَةٌ كقتل أو هزيمة قالَ قَدْ أَنْعَمَ اللَّهُ عَلَيَّ حين تخلفت إِذْ لَمْ أَكُنْ مَعَهُمْ شَهِيداً فيصيبني ما أصابهم. وَلَئِنْ أَصابَكُمْ فَضْلٌ مِنَ اللَّهِ، كنصر وغنيمة، لَيَقُولَنَّ لفرط عداوته: يا لَيْتَنِي كُنْتُ مَعَهُمْ فَأَفُوزَ فَوْزاً عَظِيماً، بالمال والعز. كأن ذلك المنافق، لم يكن بينكم وبينه مودة ولا مواصلة أصلاً، حيث يتربص الدوائر، يفرح بمصيبتكم ويتحسر بعزكم ونصركم. فإن تثاقل هذا عن القتال أو بطَّأ غيره، فَلْيُقاتِلْ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ أهلُ الإخلاص والإيمان الَّذِينَ يَشْرُونَ، أي: يبيعون الْحَياةَ الدُّنْيا بِالْآخِرَةِ، فيؤثرون الآخرة الباقية على الدنيا الفانية، وَمَنْ يُقاتِلْ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ لإعلاء كلمة الله فَيُقْتَلْ شهيدًا أَوْ يَغْلِبْ عدوه وينصره الله فَسَوْفَ
